@@ -54,13 +54,13 @@ eml_diagnostics  Diagnostic, FileId/SourceFiles, ariadne rendering (does not dep
 eml_extern       dependency-free extern table (types, effects, functions, with value `Repr`s); consulted by HIR, types, Core IR, LIR and the VM
 ```
 
-`eml_rt` (`docs/spec/object-model.md`) is the runtime under the VM (`eml_vm`, `docs/spec/vm.md`): the runtime core shared by the VM and native code (words, headers and kinds, object shapes, page allocator, local/shared RC, `share`, reuse tokens, `Heap<Fast>` / `Heap<Checked>`), plus the `String` kind: a length word followed by inline UTF-8 bytes, which Verus tracks as ghost bytes in the block permission (UTF-8 validity is proven, `string_push` appends in place to a unique left). Immortal constants live in a frozen region: a chain of read-only `Frozen<M>` chunks (`Heap::freeze` turns a builder heap into a chunk whose parent is the chunk it had attached) that every heap accepts once `Heap::attach` gives it the chain's head through an `Arc`; region objects are never written, so `Frozen` is `Sync`, and checked mode does not count them (「凍結した領域」). `Ctx<M>` is the execution context (「実行文脈 `Ctx`」): the heap, the evv, a flat `#[repr(C)]` yield record, the 19 evidence primitives and the session root table's `root_get` / `root_set` as methods (roots live outside the proven `held_ok` invariant, vouched for by the VM like stack slots; `take_roots`, and `recover`, which after a continuable runtime error copies what the roots reach into a fresh heap and drops the old one, so the failed run's words and tokens vanish, unreachable `Foreign`s are finalized and every old page returns to the `Control`; it tells heap blocks from region words by the block's page header (its `index` must point back to that page in the old heap's page list), so neither recovery nor dropping the old heap (page by page) allocates in proportion to the old heap, needs `Data` raw words written up to capacity (`alloc_data` zero-fills them), and returns `Unrecoverable` on a `Foreign`, `lin`-bit, token, shared, cyclic or non-dummy heap-immortal object, which the VM reports as `RuntimeError::Unrecovered`), proven against the evv as a sequence (spec functions over the heap, no ghost state); it shares the session's `Control` (an atomic stop word read by `Ctx::poll`, a page-counted memory budget, depth and evv limits) with every heap through an `Arc`, and `eml_rt` installs no signal handler (`eml_cli` installs the SIGINT handler with `sigaction` and without `SA_RESTART`, so a blocked `Fs.read_all` stops on Ctrl-C, and builds `eml run`'s default limits with `default_limits()`). The 25 externs are written once in the word ABI (「extern」): fixed-arity functions on raw `i64` / `Word`, Ctx-free `#[inline]` arithmetic and comparisons, and the string and I/O externs as `Ctx` methods returning `Fault` (the Arith / Output / Io / NotUtf8 texts are the runtime error texts of `docs/spec/lir.md`); results are proven for the pure ones (vstd checked arithmetic, `lex`, `decimal`, `escape`). Two more `Ctx` methods serve the REPL: `copy_string(s, max_chars)` (unsafe, proven like `println`: copies at most `max_chars` chars of a string out and releases it, counting char starts with vstd's `is_char_boundary`) and `print_line(&str)` (writes one line to `Stream::Out` and flushes). I/O goes through the session `Host` held in `Ctx` (`StdHost` buffers stdout like C stdio: line-buffered on a terminal, 64 KiB blocks otherwise; `Ctx::flush`), and `File` is a `Foreign` holding the path and a `BufRead`, read by `Fs.read_all` in 64 KiB chunks that watch the `Control`; one expected-value table (`heap/tests/expected.txt`, written from `eml_rt`'s own results by an `#[ignore]` test) pins the externs' results as a regression table. It depends only on vstd. All its code is inside `verus!` except a short list of safe glue that 「`unsafe` の核」 names (`fmt` and `Default` impls, the T4 `Drop`s, `unsafe impl Send` / `Sync`, compile-time layout and Send/Sync assertions, the checked-mode `leaks` / `check_invariants`, the `Prefix` struct declaration, constant tables and messages, `Control`'s atomic stop word and byte counter with `Ctx::poll`, and the Host side: `StopOnInterrupt` (the reader adapter that turns an `EINTR` after the stop word is set into a stop), the `Host` trait, `Opened`, `Buffering`, `StdHost`, `HostRef::new` and its `Drop`, the `Fault` / `ArithFault` / `Stop` `Display`s, and `io_reason`), and `cargo verus verify -p eml_rt` proves everything inside (memory safety on `PointsTo` permissions, heap invariants, RC against an abstract model, checked-mode promises). What Verus does not check is the named trust list T0-T11 (the 「`unsafe` の核」 section; T10 is the string byte copies, whose memory bytes equal the ghost bytes; T11 is the Host boundary, which promises nothing), pinned by `src/trust.rs`; a new FFI boundary adds a row there and a Miri test in `heap/tests/trusted.rs`.
+`eml_rt` (`docs/spec/object-model.md`) is the runtime under the VM (`eml_vm`, `docs/spec/vm.md`): the runtime core shared by the VM and native code (words, headers and kinds, object shapes, page allocator, local/shared RC, `share`, reuse tokens, `Heap<Fast>` / `Heap<Checked>`), plus the `String` kind: a length word followed by inline UTF-8 bytes, which Verus tracks as ghost bytes in the block permission (UTF-8 validity is proven, `string_push` appends in place to a unique left). Immortal constants live in a frozen region: a chain of read-only `Frozen<M>` chunks (`Heap::freeze` turns a builder heap into a chunk whose parent is the chunk it had attached) that every heap accepts once `Heap::attach` gives it the chain's head through an `Arc`; region objects are never written, so `Frozen` is `Sync`, and checked mode does not count them (「凍結した領域」). `Ctx<M>` is the execution context (「実行文脈 `Ctx`」): the heap, the evv, a flat `#[repr(C)]` yield record, the 19 evidence primitives and the session root table's `root_get` / `root_set` as methods (roots live outside the proven `held_ok` invariant, vouched for by the VM like stack slots; `take_roots`, and `recover`, which after a continuable runtime error copies what the roots reach into a fresh heap and drops the old one, so the failed run's words and tokens vanish, unreachable `Foreign`s are finalized and every old page returns to the `Control`; it tells heap blocks from region words by the block's page header (its `index` must point back to that page in the old heap's page list), so neither recovery nor dropping the old heap (page by page) allocates in proportion to the old heap, needs `Data` raw words written up to capacity (`alloc_data` zero-fills them), and returns `Unrecoverable` on a `Foreign`, `lin`-bit, token, shared, cyclic or non-dummy heap-immortal object, which the VM reports as `RuntimeError::Unrecovered`), proven against the evv as a sequence (spec functions over the heap, no ghost state); it shares the session's `Control` (an atomic stop word read by `Ctx::poll`, a page-counted memory budget, depth and evv limits) with every heap through an `Arc`, and `eml_rt` installs no signal handler (`eml_cli` installs the SIGINT handler with `sigaction` and without `SA_RESTART`, so a blocked `Fs.read_all` or standard-input read stops on Ctrl-C, and builds `eml run`'s default limits with `default_limits()`). The 38 externs are written once in the word ABI (「extern」): fixed-arity functions on raw `i64` / `Word`, Ctx-free `#[inline]` arithmetic and comparisons, and the string and I/O externs as `Ctx` methods returning `Fault` (24 bytes; the rare `NotUtf8` / `Index` payloads are boxed; the Arith / Output / Io / Stdin / NotUtf8 / Index texts are the runtime error texts of `docs/spec/lir.md`); results are proven for the pure ones (vstd checked arithmetic, `lex`, `decimal`, `escape`, and the byte-position `String` core `string_byte_length` / `string_byte_at` / `string_slice` / `string_find_from` in `heap/ctx/positions.rs`, with `slice` checking char boundaries before it allocates). The script externs (`heap/ctx/script.rs`: `write_string` for `println` / `eprintln`, `env_arg_count`, `env_arg`, `env_has`, `env_var`, `stdin_read`, `fs_write_text`, `proc_status`) go through T11 bridges that promise only that they never return `Fault::Heap`; `proc_status` takes the arguments as one string of a count and NUL-terminated arguments and checks the stop word before spawning and after the child exits. Two more `Ctx` methods serve the REPL: `copy_string(s, max_chars)` (unsafe, proven like `println`: copies at most `max_chars` chars of a string out and releases it, counting char starts with vstd's `is_char_boundary`) and `print_line(&str)` (writes one line to `Stream::Out` and flushes). I/O goes through the session `Host` held in `Ctx` (`StdHost` buffers stdout like C stdio: line-buffered on a terminal, 64 KiB blocks otherwise; `Ctx::flush`; `args` / `env_var` / `stdin` / `write_file` / `run` default to "not available" so embedders implement only output and files; `StdHost::with_args` / `with_stdin` make the `eml run` host, the REPL host has neither), and `File` is a `Foreign` holding the path and a `BufRead`, read by `Fs.read_all` in 64 KiB chunks that watch the `Control` (the standard input is read by the same `StopOnInterrupt::read_bytes`, lines via `fill_buf`); one expected-value table (`heap/tests/expected.txt`, written from `eml_rt`'s own results by an `#[ignore]` test) pins the externs' results as a regression table. It depends only on vstd. All its code is inside `verus!` except a short list of safe glue that 「`unsafe` の核」 names (`fmt` and `Default` impls, the T4 `Drop`s, `unsafe impl Send` / `Sync`, compile-time layout and Send/Sync assertions, the checked-mode `leaks` / `check_invariants`, the `Prefix` struct declaration, constant tables and messages, `Control`'s atomic stop word and byte counter with `Ctx::poll`, and the Host side: `StopOnInterrupt` (the reader adapter that turns an `EINTR` after the stop word is set into a stop) with its `read_bytes` and `Failure`, the `Host` trait with its defaults, `Opened`, `StdinLock`, `lock_stdin`, `exit_code`, `Buffering`, `StdHost`, `HostRef::new` and its `Drop`, the `Fault` / `ArithFault` / `Stop` `Display`s, and `io_reason`), and `cargo verus verify -p eml_rt` proves everything inside (memory safety on `PointsTo` permissions, heap invariants, RC against an abstract model, checked-mode promises). What Verus does not check is the named trust list T0-T11 (the 「`unsafe` の核」 section; T10 is the string byte copies, whose memory bytes equal the ghost bytes; T11 is the Host boundary, which promises nothing), pinned by `src/trust.rs`; a new FFI boundary adds a row there and a Miri test in `heap/tests/trusted.rs`.
 
 - Each stage is a pure function `fn stage(&In) -> (Out, Vec<Diagnostic>)` with no global mutable state, so it can later move onto queries (salsa).
 - Never stop on errors: `eml_cli::Session` is the only pipeline driver (the CLI, UI tests and `eml_test_support` all go through it), and each stage method (`def_map` / `lower` / `check` / `compile`) runs every stage up to its own and returns all their diagnostics, sorted. `Session` keeps no intermediate results. The parser recovers with `ERROR` nodes; type checking onward inserts an `Error` type and suppresses cascading diagnostics.
 - The parser follows rust-analyzer (event stream -> rowan tree built in `sink.rs`). A layout stage (`layout.rs`) between the lexer and the parser inserts virtual `LAYOUT_OPEN` / `LAYOUT_SEP` / `LAYOUT_CLOSE` tokens (`docs/spec/layout.md`); the parser emits no events for them, so the CST is always lossless. Grammar rules are confined to `eml_syntax/src/grammar/`.
-- From HIR onward, nodes are referenced by IDs and analysis results (types, ...) live in side tables. HIR is a `Program` of modules (module 0 is the Prelude, module 1 the entry file, then the other standard-library modules in embedded order, then the imported modules in the order the loader found them; the loader reads files through a `ModuleSource`); items use program-wide `ItemId`s (module + local index, side tables are `ItemMap`), while body nodes use per-body arena IDs (`ExprId`, etc.). HIR nodes carry a `TextRange` (not a `SyntaxNodePtr`), because some expressions, such as reassociated operator subexpressions, have no syntax node. Records are nominal: a `data` constructor may name its fields (`Constructor::field_names`), and field names are not in any namespace. HIR keeps construction as `ExprKind::Record` (fields in source order with their declared index; `A {}` becomes a constructor `Path`), updates as `ExprKind::Update` and projections (`p.name`, `t.0`) as `ExprKind::Field` with the field still a name or index, record patterns as `PatKind::Con` in declared order, and section `(.f)` as a lambda over a hidden parameter; `Body::omitted_fields` marks the `_` of fields a pattern left out and `Body::use_lambdas` the lambdas `use` built (`docs/implementation/architecture.md`).
-- Built-ins are `extern` declarations backed by one table in `eml_extern`. The standard library lives in the repository's `std/` tree (`Prelude.em`, `Fs.em`), embedded into the binary as `eml_hir::STD`; its modules have canonical names `Prelude` and `Std.<Name>` (the root `Std` is reserved) and display paths `<std>/Fs.em`, and every program loads all of them. The Prelude also declares `List` (constructors `Nil` and `::`), `Option` and `Result`; the list syntax `[…]` names the Prelude's `Nil` and `::` through `eml_hir`'s `LangItems` without name lookup, so a user's own `Nil` does not change it. Only standard-library modules may write `extern` (E1033 otherwise), including `extern m` inside an `instance`, which binds a method to the row `<module>.<Class> <Type>.<method>` (`Prelude.Eq Int.==`). `IO` is a label-only extern effect: extern functions run in place (`Rhs::Extern`, no handler, no evidence, no continuation frame). Table rows and `std/` are tied together by a test in `eml_hir` (every row is named by exactly one extern function or instance `extern`). `Repr` lives in `eml_extern` (re-exported by `eml_lir`): type rows carry a `repr`, which translate reads, and function rows carry `params` and `ret`; `crates/eml_core_ir/tests/externs.rs` ties them to the `std/` signatures through `eml_core_ir::type_repr`.
+- From HIR onward, nodes are referenced by IDs and analysis results (types, ...) live in side tables. HIR is a `Program` of modules (module 0 is the Prelude, module 1 the entry file, then the modules in the order the loader found them through imports and qualifiers, std and user modules interleaved; the loader reads files through a `ModuleSource`); items use program-wide `ItemId`s (module + local index, side tables are `ItemMap`), while body nodes use per-body arena IDs (`ExprId`, etc.). HIR nodes carry a `TextRange` (not a `SyntaxNodePtr`), because some expressions, such as reassociated operator subexpressions, have no syntax node. Records are nominal: a `data` constructor may name its fields (`Constructor::field_names`), and field names are not in any namespace. HIR keeps construction as `ExprKind::Record` (fields in source order with their declared index; `A {}` becomes a constructor `Path`), updates as `ExprKind::Update` and projections (`p.name`, `t.0`) as `ExprKind::Field` with the field still a name or index, record patterns as `PatKind::Con` in declared order, and section `(.f)` as a lambda over a hidden parameter; `Body::omitted_fields` marks the `_` of fields a pattern left out and `Body::use_lambdas` the lambdas `use` built (`docs/implementation/architecture.md`).
+- Built-ins are `extern` declarations backed by one table in `eml_extern`. The standard library lives in the repository's `std/` tree (`Prelude.em`, `Fs.em`, `List.em`, `String.em`, `Env.em`, `Stdin.em`, `Proc.em`; `docs/spec/stdlib.md`), embedded into the binary as `eml_hir::STD`; its modules have canonical names `Prelude` and `Std.<Name>` (the root `Std` is reserved) and display paths `<std>/Fs.em`. The Prelude is always loaded; another std module `X` is loaded only when a loaded module imports it, a user module writes an import whose path is `X` (even when the user's `X.em` wins), or `X` / `Std.X` appears as a qualifier anywhere in a user module's syntax (`Parse::paths`), so `ExternIndex::ty` returns `Option` (Prelude rows panic when missing), and the REPL's E2018 check skips std items the current input did not load. Externs pass only scalars, strings and `Foreign`s; std eml code wraps sentinels into `List` / `Option`. The Prelude also declares `List` (constructors `Nil` and `::`), `Option` and `Result`; the list syntax `[…]` names the Prelude's `Nil` and `::` through `eml_hir`'s `LangItems` without name lookup, so a user's own `Nil` does not change it. Only standard-library modules may write `extern` (E1033 otherwise), including `extern m` inside an `instance`, which binds a method to the row `<module>.<Class> <Type>.<method>` (`Prelude.Eq Int.==`). `IO` is a label-only extern effect: extern functions run in place (`Rhs::Extern`, no handler, no evidence, no continuation frame). Table rows and `std/` are tied together by a test in `eml_hir` (every row is named by exactly one extern function or instance `extern`). `Repr` lives in `eml_extern` (re-exported by `eml_lir`): type rows carry a `repr`, which translate reads, and function rows carry `params` and `ret`; `crates/eml_core_ir/tests/externs.rs` ties them to the `std/` signatures through `eml_core_ir::type_repr`.
 - Types after checking are `TypeId`s into one program-wide hash-consed `eml_types::TypeStore` (`TypedProgram.types`); `TypeStore::intern` is crate-private; later stages add types only through `TypeStore::substitute` (monomorphization). Exported types carry linearity: `TypeKind::Fn` has `lin: Lin` (`Unr`, `Lin`, `Poly`, or `Var(u32)`, a declaration's Kind variable), `Flexible(Lin)` and `OpVar(_, Lin)` carry a Kind, and `TypeStore::linearity(ty)` gives a type's value (a debug assertion fires on declaration-relative types whose value depends on a `Rigid` or `Var`). `check_module` goes through call-graph SCCs from the callees up, checking the bodies, solving the SCC's Kinds, and only then exporting the bodies' types (`Exporter::solved`; `Exporter::diagnostic` is the pre-solve export for messages and prints no linearity), so a body's types depend on its SCC's solution. `TypedProgram::kind_values(decl, args, store)` evaluates a declaration's `Var`s at an instance (the argument of `Substitution::new(type_args, kind_values)`; `Substitution::partial` leaves `Var` alone), `uniform_kind(function, position)` gives the Kind of a uniform-position or entry `Flexible`, and `needs_substitution(decl)` says whether a body must be copied (`docs/spec/types.md`, "Kind の書き出し"). Only `Lin` is public; `Linearity`, `Multiplicity` and `ArrowLin` stay inside `eml_types`. Tuples are label-free `Tuple(Vec<_>)` in `TyShape`, `TypeKind` and `ShapeTy` (`Unit` is the empty tuple). Projections and updates resolve their field from the type known at that point (`check/field.rs`; E2013 when it is still a variable, E2014 when there is no such field; only single-constructor types and tuples have projections), and the results go to the side tables `BodyTypes::fields`, `updates` and `discarded` (the fields a projection or update drops, which the linearity pass treats like `_`). Lambda arguments of a call are checked after its other arguments (`docs/spec/types.md`), so `map (.name) people` needs no annotation. Walks over inference-table types visit each union-find representative once (the `Walk` guard in `eml_types/src/table/mod.rs`), and `Exporter` memoizes per type, so shared types cost their store size, not their unfolded tree size.
 - Two IR layers with separate types. Core IR (`docs/spec/core-ir.md`) is the semantic layer: statements `let` / `unpack`, terminators `return` / `jump` / `switch`, effect instructions `handle` / `perform` / `resume` and call `mask`s, and no RC, `box` or tail calls. LIR (`docs/spec/lir.md`, crate `eml_lir`) is Core IR after evidence passing, copied by `eml_core_ir::to_lir` with the same names and numbers: no effect instructions or `mask`s, but `dup` / `decref` / `release`, `box` / `unbox` and terminator `tail`. Both are lists of basic blocks per function with block parameters and forward edges only (rules R1-R8, written once in `docs/spec/lir.md`; Core IR follows the unmarked parts) and a `Repr` (`obj`, `tobj`, `int`, `enum`, `unit`) per variable. Every variable and every function's `ret` also has a Kind mark (`eml_lir::Mark { Unr, Lin, Poly }`: `VarInfo.mark`, `ret_mark`; `unr` may be dupped and decref'd, `lin` and `poly` may not), decided in translate from `TypeStore::linearity` (`linearity_or_poly` for types read without the instance substitution), by EP and boxing for the variables they add (boxing adds `unr` scalars and copies the original marks for `f$boxed` and re-received parameters), and never changed by later passes. The text form writes the mark after the Repr only when it is not `unr` (`x.1: tobj lin`, `-> tobj poly`, `ask(unit) -> tobj poly`); writing `unr` is a parse error. The extern rows (`param_kinds` / `ret_kind`), the primitive table (`param_marks` / `ret_mark`) and the operation table (`OperationInfo::param_marks` / `ret_mark`) have mark columns. The shared types (`Atom`, `VarInfo`, `Layout`, `Ctor`, `Prim`, `EffectInfo`, ..., and `Repr` re-exported from `eml_extern`) live in `eml_lir` and are not re-exported by `eml_core_ir`; `eml_lir::syntax` and `eml_lir::check` hold the text-form and verifier parts both IRs share. Uniform positions (`apply` operands and results, type-variable and tuple fields) are `tobj` (a `clause` call instead takes the evidence as `obj` and the operation's declared `params` / `ret` from the effect table), and a function used as a value must be uniform (every parameter and its `ret` compatible with `tobj`); `box` / `unbox` convert the boxed scalars (`int`, `enum`; `Repr::BOXED_SCALARS`) to and from `tobj`, while `unit` and `obj` pass without an instruction (`Repr::compatible`; the 位置の規則 section of `docs/spec/lir.md`). `Program.layouts` is the data layout table (one layout per data type the IR names, one per tuple size, in first-use order; field `Repr`s from the declared types): `con` / `unpack` / `release` carry a `Ctor` (layout id and tag) and a `switch` with tag cases carries its layout id. The verifier checks every instruction against its layout (R8) and extern calls against the rows' `Repr`s (R7), but not which layout built a value; the VM reads only the tag (checked mode also checks the tag and field count). `switch`, `unpack`, `unbox` and a primitive's borrowed arguments (`PrimRow::borrowed`, e.g. the evidence of `ev_clause`; `Rhs::for_each_consumed` leaves them out) only read their value. Fields start borrowed and stay borrowed while only read; Perceus takes ownership of a field only where it is needed (consumed, live at a jump target, live after its owner's last reference is consumed, or live after a call while its owner is retained, so a retained owner never crosses a call), by `dup` when the owner is still needed there and otherwise by resolving the owner with `release` from the parent down (which frees a unique box and hands its references to the kept fields); a retained owner is decref'd once its last borrowed field dies (the Perceus section of `docs/spec/lir.md`). So a `Lin` field is never dupped, and in checked mode `eml_rt` reports a `dup` of an object with the `lin` bit (a `File`) as a heap error (`HeapError::LinDup`, "a Lin object is shared"). `eml_core_ir::lower(hir, typed, entry, &SourceFiles) -> eml_lir::Program` (and `lower_until(.., eml_lir::Pass)`) runs `Pass::{Translate, Evidence}` (`core_until` stops there and returns Core IR), then `to_lir`, then `eml_lir::passes_until`, which runs `eml_lir::Pass::{Boxing, Contract, Perceus}`; each crate's `pipeline.rs` knows only its own order. Debug builds also run three linearity checks in translate (`docs/spec/core-ir.md`): `check_linearity` in `translate/instances.rs` compares each reference with its callee instance, each `perform` with its operation's declared type, and each handler clause with the operation (`TypeStore::lin_compatible` rejects an `unr` meeting a `lin`), and `check_captures` in `translate/expr.rs` requires that an `unr` closure captures only `Unr` values; a body that translate does not copy is asserted to hold no `Var`. Verifiers run in debug builds: Core IR has two levels (`verify_translated` after translate, which rejects `prim` / `clause` before EP; `verify_evidence` after evidence, which rejects `handle` / `perform` / `resume` / `mask`), LIR has three (`verify_lowered`, run by `passes_until` on its input and reported as `invalid LIR before boxing`, rejects `tail`, `box`, `unbox` and RC instructions; `verify_scopes` after boxing and contract adds the boundary checks of calls, function values and `tobj` fields; `verify` after Perceus adds ownership). The mark checks are V2 (sender and receiver marks are compatible: only an `unr` meeting a `lin` fails, `poly` meets both, constants meet any; run wherever Repr compatibility is compared, so Core IR and the LIR entry level check only `jump` and `return`, and `perform` / `handle` / `resume` are never compared; binders of a `never` result are exempt, a `clause` call's evidence argument and a `clause` function's evidence parameter are `unr`), V1 (LIR ownership level only: no `dup` or `decref` of a non-`unr` variable) and the build check (a `con` or `closure` binder's mark is at least the join of its arguments' marks, `unr` < `poly` < `lin`, at every level). Compatibility is not transitive, so contract's tail-call formation and translate's `jump` forwarding also require compatible (forwarding: equal) marks. `unpack` and case field marks are trusted (layouts carry no Kind). `drop x` (release one reference, not "destroy") is a consuming rhs that Perceus adds nothing around: EP emits it for the dead yield result `x` on slow paths (a dummy) and in `prompt$` and the `compose$` / `mask$` slow paths, and for `ret` on the abort path of `prompt$` (the one real cleanup: it closes a captured `File`); translate emits `drop v` at a decision-tree leaf that takes apart a non-`unr` value and binds no non-`unr` variable out of it. A non-`unr` `con` variable is opened by the decision tree (its arguments read directly) only when the scrutinee is its only use (a let-bound local referenced exactly once) and the decision is static or no arm receives it; `materialize_once` merges only `unr` occurrences. Hence Perceus emits only `release` on a non-`unr` variable. Translate lowers each (function, type arguments, row bits) instance that `translate/instances.rs` collects from the entry (names `f@[Int]`, with arrow linearity shown as `%1` for `Lin` and `%m` for `Poly` (`id@[Int %1 -> Int]`, so instances differing only in linearity get distinct names); row bits set for row variables that may yield written `twice<e>`, uniform positions `_`, polymorphic recursion made uniform per type-variable position; the row bits come from `eml_types`' per-reference `Instantiation::rows: Vec<RowYield>`; the uniform positions are computed by `eml_types` after type checking, `TypedProgram::uniform`, which also reports E2012), resolves each method reference through `eml_types::resolve` to an instance method, a class default, an extern row, or a function generated for a derived or tuple instance (`translate/derive.rs`; names like `"Show Color.show"`, quoted in the IR text like extern names with spaces), builds blocks forward (`FnBuilder`), makes case-of-case itself, forwards jumps to return-only blocks, marks lifted and helper functions `internal`, marks functions and `apply`s that may yield `yields` (splitting an `apply` after each arrow that may yield), and gives extern calls source positions; translate alone emits `handle` / `perform` / `resume` and call `mask`s. Evidence (`evidence/`, `docs/spec/core-ir.md` evidence passing) replaces them: a context `evv` (persistent list of evidence objects, innermost first) and a yield state replace handler frames (the primitives and the runtime model are in `docs/spec/lir.md`); `perform` becomes `prim ev_lookup` + `prim ev_clause` + a `clause E.op` call; each clause becomes an in-place entry function (`clause` mark): immediately resuming clauses with no yield point run in place, others start a yield (`yield_to`, or `yield_abort` for `never`); each non-tail yield point in a `yields` function gets a `prim yielding` check whose slow path composes a continuation closure `f$kN` (join blocks reached become `f$jJ`); helper functions `id$` / `compose$` / `prompt$` / `resume$` / `rewind$` / `mask$[…]` are not `internal`; a yielding function returns a dummy (`prim dummy()`, `0`, `#0`, `()`) that `box` / `unbox` / `dup` / `decref` / `drop` must pass through. Boxing fixes the whole-program ABI (T3 raises a scalar `ret` to `tobj` when a tail-position call's result would need a conversion; a `never` operation's `clause` returns nothing, so T3, boxing and the verifier never compare its result and contract makes a tail one a `tail clause`; value-only internal functions become uniform in place, `clause`-marked functions conform to their operation's ABI, other functions used as values get a uniform `f$boxed`) and inserts `box` / `unbox`; contract removes unused pure `let`s and is the only pass that forms tail calls, when the call's result is compatible with the caller's `ret`. The VM keeps each task's frames on a stack of one-word slots (a two-word header per frame; the bottom frame returns to `Finish`), primitives are `Ctx` methods (their table, `Prim::row`, in `eml_lir`) and do not release their borrowed arguments, `clause` runs like `apply`, and `prim dummy()` is the heap's immortal dummy object (a zero `BigInt`) that RC instructions leave alone (`docs/spec/lir.md`, 「実行の意味」). Runtime errors from an extern call print `{fault}` and `  at path:line:column`. Instance names depend only on the type arguments, so a function's code never depends on the rest of the program: a dedicated name table writes types and classes (Prelude unqualified, entry and session items by middle name, `Main.` on a Prelude collision, other modules qualified), Kinds of uniform positions show (`_%m`), and beyond 64 characters the name is a 64-bit structural hash `f@#<16 hex>`. REPL session items get middle names (`f`, or `f#3` when an earlier input defined the name), session instance methods are named from the name table, and the shared `op$` / `con$` / `cont$` / `cont$state` are never called directly (pinned by a test), so boxing always makes them uniform in place. For a REPL expression input whose value is written, the collector also gathers display functions breadth-first from the input's type (`display$N$k`, named by the input number N and the discovery order k, cut at depth 100 and at 1000 functions; lists add `display$N$k$rest`), and `entry$input$N` returns the value's text instead of printing it (`docs/spec/core-ir.md`, 「表示関数」).
 - Diagnostic codes are defined in a per-stage `codes` module (e.g. `eml_syntax::codes`, E0xxx). E0004 (not yet supported) is used by every stage, so it lives in `eml_diagnostics` (`NOT_YET_SUPPORTED`, `Diagnostic::not_yet_supported`).
@@ -71,10 +71,10 @@ eml_extern       dependency-free extern table (types, effects, functions, with v
 
 - Work test-first (TDD). Snapshots use `insta`, mostly inline (`@"..."`).
 - Each crate's integration tests build into one binary, `integration` (`autotests = false`; `tests/main.rs` declares every file as a module). A file not declared there does not run. Libs set `doctest = false`, and libs without unit tests also set `test = false` (`docs/implementation/testing.md`).
-- UI tests (`crates/eml_cli/tests/ui.rs`): `tests/ui/run/**/*.em` must run to completion and `tests/ui/run-fail/**/*.em` must compile cleanly and end in a runtime error, and `tests/ui/check-fail/**/*.em` must produce at least one error; output is snapshotted. Pass/fail expectation is decided by the top-level directory, and every test sits in a category subdirectory below it (`docs/implementation/testing.md`). A test is one file `<top>/<category>/<name>.em` or one directory `<top>/<category>/<name>/main.em` whose other `.em` files are the modules it imports; the harness fails on any other layout. `run` / `run-fail` tests run on the VM in checked mode with `default_limits()` (stdout and error text are snapshotted), then in fast mode, which must give the same stdout and the same outcome; in `run-fail`, fast mode runs only when the checked error is one fast mode also reports (not Leak, `VmFault::Internal` or `VmFault::Extern(Fault::Heap(_))`) (「2つのモードで流す」).
-- REPL transcripts (`crates/eml_cli/tests/repl.rs`, `docs/implementation/testing.md` 「REPL のトランスクリプト」): `crates/eml_cli/tests/repl/<category>/<name>.repl`, or `<category>/<name>/main.repl` with the `.em` modules it imports. `> ` starts an input, `. ` continues it, and a bare `.` is the blank line that ends a definition. The harness checks `input_status` on every prefix of every input, runs the transcript in checked mode against a file snapshot (inputs, output, `full_with_paths` diagnostics, runtime errors, the `close` result) and again in fast mode with the same per-input output. The insta glob lock is shared with the UI tests (`tests/common`).
+- UI tests (`crates/eml_cli/tests/ui.rs`): `tests/ui/run/**/*.em` must run to completion and `tests/ui/run-fail/**/*.em` must compile cleanly and end in a runtime error, and `tests/ui/check-fail/**/*.em` must produce at least one error; output is snapshotted. Pass/fail expectation is decided by the top-level directory, and every test sits in a category subdirectory below it (`docs/implementation/testing.md`). A test is one file `<top>/<category>/<name>.em` or one directory `<top>/<category>/<name>/main.em` whose other `.em` files are the modules it imports; the harness fails on any other layout. a test's `<name>.args` / `.stdin` / `.env` side files feed `CaptureHost` (`eml_vm`; memory-backed `Fs.write_text`, whose files are appended to the snapshot as `--- written: path ---`, real children for `Proc.run` with their stdout captured), and `run` / `run-fail` tests run on the VM in checked mode with `default_limits()` (stdout and error text are snapshotted), then in fast mode, which must give the same stdout and the same outcome; in `run-fail`, fast mode runs only when the checked error is one fast mode also reports (not Leak, `VmFault::Internal` or `VmFault::Extern(Fault::Heap(_))`) (「2つのモードで流す」).
+- REPL transcripts (`crates/eml_cli/tests/repl.rs`, `docs/implementation/testing.md` 「REPL のトランスクリプト」): `crates/eml_cli/tests/repl/<category>/<name>.repl`, or `<category>/<name>/main.repl` with the `.em` modules it imports. `> ` starts an input, `. ` continues it, and a bare `.` is the blank line that ends a definition. The harness uses a `CaptureHost` without stdin, checks `input_status` on every prefix of every input, runs the transcript in checked mode against a file snapshot (inputs, output, `full_with_paths` diagnostics, runtime errors, the `close` result) and again in fast mode with the same per-input output. The insta glob lock is shared with the UI tests (`tests/common`).
 - `eml_rt` (`docs/implementation/testing.md`, 「`eml_rt` の検証」): unit tests take the mode as a type parameter and `both_modes!` runs them in fast and checked mode; internal functions get `Tracked::assume_new()` for their permissions. Miri runs only the `trusted` module (the trust list's "Miri で確かめること"), not the whole suite, and tests do not shrink under `cfg!(miri)`. A change to `eml_rt` must pass `cargo verus verify -p eml_rt` and `verusfmt --verus-only --check` besides the usual gate.
-- `crates/eml_cli/tests/cli.rs` checks the binary's exit codes (0 success / 1 diagnostic or runtime error / 2 usage error / 130 interrupted; the two `eml run` SIGINT tests are `cfg(unix)`); `tests/terminal.rs` runs `eml repl` with piped stdin (three `cfg(unix)` SIGINT tests), `tests/lines.rs` tests `eml_cli::lines`, and `tests/pretty.rs` tests `eml_cli::pretty`.
+- `crates/eml_cli/tests/cli.rs` checks the binary's exit codes (0 success / 1 diagnostic or runtime error / 2 usage error / 130 interrupted; the four `eml run` SIGINT tests are `cfg(unix)`; `eml FILE ARGS...` runs a script directly, passing every later argument); `tests/terminal.rs` runs `eml repl` with piped stdin (three `cfg(unix)` SIGINT tests), `tests/lines.rs` tests `eml_cli::lines`, and `tests/pretty.rs` tests `eml_cli::pretty`.
 - Benchmarks (`docs/implementation/benchmarks.md`): the programs in `bench/` have their `RunStats` pinned by inline snapshots in `crates/eml_vm/tests/bench.rs` (checked mode), so a change in work counts shows up as a snapshot diff (an expected-value change). `bench/run.sh` (macOS only) measures `instructions retired` of `eml run` (the VM in fast mode) with `/usr/bin/time -l` on a release build and prints each program's execution part (minus `empty`); record its output in `benchmarks.md` at the end of a stage.
 - Test changes come in three kinds (`docs/implementation/testing.md`): (1) pass/fail changes (moving a test between `run`, `run-fail` and `check-fail`, deleting a test, UI or unit) are listed one by one in the work's spec; (2) expected-value changes (UI output, diagnostic codes or wording, stage-dump snapshots such as Core IR, moving a UI test without changing pass/fail) are described as a scope in the work's spec (e.g. "rewrite every UI test that uses E3005"); approving the spec is the agreement for kinds 1 and 2, and the reason goes in the spec and the commit message; (3) mechanical follow-ups that keep every expected value byte-identical need no agreement, and the commit message is the record.
 - Never bend the design to keep existing tests unchanged (no parallel enum variants, flags, test-only fields, or spec exceptions for that purpose). Put the test change in the work's spec instead, stating its kind.
````

- [ ] **Step 4: 計測の記録を書く**

`docs/implementation/benchmarks.md` の末尾に、Step 3 で測った値で次の節を足す。値はこのブランチで測ったものに置き換え、比べた結果の文もその値に合わせる。

```markdown
### S12a

起動と標準ライブラリの核を入れたときの費用を `bench/run.sh` で測った。

- 機種: Mac16,1 (Apple M4)
- OS: macOS 27.0.1 (26A434)
- rustc: rustc 1.98.1 (48a229cea 2026-09-01)

変える前 (コミット ae5c57d、main)。

| プログラム | instructions retired | 実行の分 | 実時間 (s) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `empty` | 37113913 | | 0.00 |
| `fib` | 121457973 | 84344060 | 0.00 |
| `list` | 298415312 | 261301399 | 0.01 |
| `loop` | 201001431 | 163887518 | 0.00 |
| `state` | 206303164 | 169189251 | 0.01 |
| `tree` | 260625701 | 223511788 | 0.01 |

変えた後。

| プログラム | instructions retired | 実行の分 | 実時間 (s) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `empty` | 38668032 | | 0.00 |
| `fib` | 123641847 | 84973815 | 0.00 |
| `list` | 302730800 | 264062768 | 0.01 |
| `loop` | 203607278 | 164939246 | 0.00 |
| `state` | 211847002 | 173178970 | 0.01 |
| `tree` | 263431800 | 224763768 | 0.01 |

`empty` は約155万 (約4.2%) 増えた。使わない `Fs` を読まなくなったので、読み込みの変更 (Task 1) だけの時点では `eml check bench/empty.em` が約14万減っていた。増えた分の内訳は次のとおりである。

- Prelude に足した `eprintln` の宣言で、`eml check bench/empty.em` が約65万増える。`eprintln` の2行を除くと、`check` は約 3860 万から約 3795 万になる
- 残りは起動の費用で、バイナリが約14.5万バイト (約2.8%) 大きくなったことによる。`eml --version` (起動と clap の読みだけ) が、約 2310 万から約 2500 万に増えた。CLI の変更 (`eml FILE ARGS...` の読み替え) を元に戻しても `--version` は変わらなかったので、引数の読み方の費用ではない

実行の分は、`state` で約2.4%、`list` で約1.1%、`fib` で約0.7%、`loop` で約0.6%、`tree` で約0.6% 増えた。仕事の回数 (`crates/eml_vm/tests/bench.rs` の `RunStats`) は変わらない。`state` は `println` と算術のほかに extern を使わないので、VM の `extern_call` の行の束縛 (`let ext`) を戻して測ったが、`state` は変わらなかった。ほかの原因は調べていない。
```
